Cloudflare&#39;s architecture as customer zero&quot;],&quot;description&quot;:[0,&quot;In our post about Project Glasswing, we made the argument that the architecture around a vulnerability matters more than the speed of the patch. Here we walk through what that architecture looks like, the threats it defends against, and how we run it ourselves as Cloudflare&#39;s customer zero.&quot;],&quot;imgPreview&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7IDIlD55jcZH6evYpTDert/9c6865086c66d5947ee83c5d1a330ac8/OG_Share_2024-2025-2026__38_.png&quot;]}],&quot;publicly_index&quot;:[0,true]}],[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;xrcYtr7kU54LNDB8MEmQY&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Project Glasswing: what Mythos showed us&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;cyber-frontier-models&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;In recent weeks, we pointed Mythos and other security-focused LLMs at live code across critical parts of our infrastructure. We share what we observed, the modelsâ strengths and weaknesses, and what the work around them needs to look like before any of it can scale.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For the last few months, we&amp;#39;ve been testing a range of security-focused LLMs on our own infrastructure. These LLMs  help identify potential vulnerabilities in our own systems, so we can fix them â and they also show us what attackers are going to be able to do with the latest models.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;None of these LLMs has captured more attention than Mythos Preview, from Anthropic. A few weeks ago, we were invited to use Mythos Preview as part of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.anthropic.com/glasswing\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Project Glasswing&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We soon pointed it at more than fifty of our own repositories â to see what it would find, and to see how it works. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This post shares what we observed, what the models did well and what they didn&amp;#39;t, and how the architecture and process around them needs to change, so they can be used at scale.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-changed-with-mythos-preview\&quot;&gt;What changed with Mythos Preview&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-changed-with-mythos-preview\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Mythos Preview is a real step forward, and it&amp;#39;s worth saying that plainly before getting into anything else. We&amp;#39;ve been running models against our code for a while now, and the jump from what was possible with previous general-purpose frontier models to what Mythos Preview does today is not just a refinement of what came before.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;It&amp;#39;s a different kind of tool doing a different kind of work, and that makes a clean apples-to-apples comparison to earlier models difficult. So rather than trying to benchmark Mythos Preview against general-purpose frontier models, it&amp;#39;s more useful to describe what it can actually do, and two features that stood out across the work we did with Mythos Preview:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Exploit chain construction -&lt;/b&gt; A real attack rarely uses one bug. It chains several small attack primitives together into a working exploit. For instance, it might turn a use-after-free bug into an arbitrary read and write primitive, hijack the control flow, and use return-oriented programming (ROP) chains to take full control over a system. Mythos Preview can take several of these primitives and reason about how to combine them into a working proof. The reasoning it shows along the way looks like the work of a senior researcher rather than the output of an automated scanner.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Proof generation -&lt;/b&gt; Finding a bug and proving it&amp;#39;s exploitable are two different things, and Mythos Preview can do both. It writes code that would trigger the suspected bug, compiles that code in a scratch environment, and runs it. If the program does what the model expected, that&amp;#39;s the proof. If it doesn&amp;#39;t, the model reads the failure, adjusts its hypothesis, and tries again. The loop matters as much as the bugs it finds, because a suspected flaw without a working proof is speculation, and Mythos Preview closes that gap on its own.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Some of what we describe above is not entirely unique to Mythos Preview. When we ran other frontier models through the same harness, they found a fair number of the same underlying bugs, and in some cases they got further than we expected on the reasoning side too. Where they fell short was at the point of stitching the pieces together. A model would identify an interesting bug, write a thoughtful description of why it mattered, and then stop, leaving the actual chain unfinished and the question of exploitability open. What changed with Mythos Preview is that a model can now take those low-severity bugs (which would traditionally sit invisible in a backlog) and chain them into a single, more severe exploit.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;model-refusals-in-legitimate-vulnerability-research\&quot;&gt;Model refusals in legitimate vulnerability research&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#model-refusals-in-legitimate-vulnerability-research\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Mythos Preview model provided by Anthropic, as part of Project Glasswing, did not have the additional safeguards that are present in generally available models (like Opus 4.7 or GPT-5.5).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Despite this, the model organically pushes back on certain requests - much like the cyber capabilities that made it useful for vulnerability hunting, the model has its own emergent guardrails that sometimes cause it to push back on legitimate security research requests. But as we found, these organic refusals arenât consistent - the same task, framed differently or presented in a different context, could produce completely different outcomes as illustrated in the examples below.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5St6rLRq9wkuxwmLHZ88CV/b4eb948c917ef7f7b0028ccc8ec0aefe/image2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1242\&quot; height=\&quot;716\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Example of Mythos Preview pushing back on building a working proof of conceptÂ &lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For example, the model initially refused to do vulnerability research on a project, then agreed to perform the same research on the same code after an unrelated change to the projectâs environment. Nothing about the code being analyzed had changed. \n\nIn another case, the model found and confirmed several serious memory bugs in a codebase, and then refused to write a demonstration exploit. The same request, framed differently, got a different answer, and even the same request can produce different outcomes across runs due to the probabilistic nature of the model. Semantically equivalent tasks can produce opposite outcomes depending on how and when theyâre presented to the model.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This matters because while the modelâs organic refusals/guardrails are real, they arenât consistent enough to serve as a complete safety boundary on their own. Thatâs precisely why any capable cyber frontier model made generally available in the future must include additional safeguards on top of this baseline behavior - making it appropriate for broader use outside of a controlled research context like Project Glasswing.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;the-signal-to-noise-problem\&quot;&gt;The signal-to-noise problem&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-signal-to-noise-problem\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One of the hardest parts of triaging security vulnerabilities is deciding which bugs are real, which are exploitable, and which need fixing now. This was a hard problem even in the pre-AI world. AI vulnerability scanners and AI-generated code have made it worse, and at Cloudflare we&amp;#39;ve built multiple post-validation stages to deal with it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Two factors dominate the noise rate:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Programming language&lt;/b&gt; - C and C++ give you direct memory control and, with it, bug classes - buffer overflows, out-of-bounds reads and writes - that memory-safe languages like Rust eliminate at compile time. We saw consistently more false positives from projects written in memory-unsafe languages.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Model bias&lt;/b&gt; - A good human researcher tells you what they found and how confident they are. Models don&amp;#39;t. Ask a model to find bugs, and it will find them, whether the code has any or not. Findings come back hedged with &amp;quot;possibly,&amp;quot; &amp;quot;potentially,&amp;quot; &amp;quot;could in theory,&amp;quot; and the hedged findings vastly outnumber the solid ones. That&amp;#39;s a reasonable bias for an exploratory tool. It&amp;#39;s a ruinous one for a triage queue, where every speculative finding spends human attention and tokens to dismiss, and that cost compounds across thousands of findings.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Mythos Preview represents a clear improvement here, particularly in its ability to chain primitives - combining multiple vulnerabilities into a working proof of concept rather than reporting them in isolation. A finding that arrives with a PoC is a finding you can act on, and it means far less time spent asking &amp;quot;is this even real?&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our harnesses are deliberately tuned to over-report, so we see more (and miss less), which comes with a lot more noise. But at triage time, Mythos Preview&amp;#39;s output has noticeably higher quality: fewer hedged findings, clearer reproduction steps, and less work to reach a fix-or-dismiss decision.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;why-pointing-a-generic-coding-agent-at-a-repo-doesnt-work\&quot;&gt;Why pointing a generic coding agent at a repo doesn&amp;#39;t work&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#why-pointing-a-generic-coding-agent-at-a-repo-doesnt-work\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When we first started AI-assisted vulnerability research last year, our instinct was the obvious one: point a generic coding agent at an arbitrary repository and ask it to discover vulnerabilities. This approach works, in the sense that the model will produce findings, but it doesn&amp;#39;t work in producing meaningful coverage of a real codebase and identifying findings of value. There are two main reasons for this:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Context -&lt;/b&gt; Coding agents are tuned for one focused stream of work: building a feature, fixing a bug, writing a refactor. They ingest a lot of source code, hold a single hypothesis at a time, and iterate against it. That&amp;#39;s exactly the wrong shape for vulnerability research, which is narrow and parallel by nature. A human researcher picks one specific thing to look at and investigates it thoroughly. That one thing might be a single complex feature, transitions across security boundaries, or a specific vulnerability class like command injections, where attacker input ends up being run as a shell command. Then they do it again, for a different feature, security boundary, or vulnerability class, several thousand times across the codebase. A single agent session (even with subagents) against a hundred-thousand-line repository can cover maybe a tenth of a percent of the surface in a useful way before the model&amp;#39;s context window fills up and compaction kicks in - potentially discarding earlier findings that would have mattered.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Throughput -&lt;/b&gt; A single-stream agent does one thing at a time, but real codebases need many hypotheses against many components at once, with the ability to fan out further when something interesting turns up. You can drive a single agent harder, but at some point you stop being limited by the model and start being limited by the shape of the interaction itself. Using the model directly in a coding agent turns out to be fine for manual investigation when a researcher already has a lead and wants a second pair of eyes. However, it&amp;#39;s the wrong tool for achieving high coverage. Once we accepted that, we stopped trying to make Mythos Preview do the wrong job and started building the harness around it instead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-a-harness-actually-fixes\&quot;&gt;What a harness actually fixes&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-a-harness-actually-fixes\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Four lessons came out of running the work at scale, and each one pointed to the need for a harness that manages the overall execution:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Narrow scope produces better findings -&lt;/b&gt; Telling the model &amp;quot;Find vulnerabilities in this repository&amp;quot; makes it wander. Telling it &amp;quot;Look for command injection in this specific function, with this trust boundary above it, here&amp;#39;s the architecture document and here&amp;#39;s prior coverage of this area&amp;quot; makes it do something much closer to what a researcher would actually do.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Adversarial review reduces noise -&lt;/b&gt; Adding a second agent between the initial finding and the queue - one with a different prompt, a different model, and no ability to generate its own findings - catches a lot of the noise that the first agent would miss if it just checked its own work. It turns out that putting two agents in deliberate disagreement is way more effective than just telling one agent to be careful.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Splitting the chain across agents produces better reasoning -&lt;/b&gt; Asking &amp;quot;Is this code buggy?&amp;quot; and &amp;quot;Can an attacker actually reach this bug from outside the system?&amp;quot; are two different questions, and the model is better at each one when you ask them separately, because each question is narrower than the combined version.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Parallel narrow tasks beat one exhaustive agent -&lt;/b&gt; Coverage improves when many agents work on tightly scoped questions and we deduplicate the results afterward, rather than asking one agent to be exhaustive.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each of those observations is about model behavior, and put together they describe something that isn&amp;#39;t a chat interface anymore. It&amp;#39;s a harness that helps you achieve the final outcomes. The first steps to building a harness are simple, as you can ask the model to help, which is what we did. We used Mythos Preview to build on, tailor, and improve our original harnesses to suit its strengths.\n\nAn example of what a harness looks like in practice is described below.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;our-vulnerability-discovery-harness\&quot;&gt;Our vulnerability discovery harness&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#our-vulnerability-discovery-harness\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Here&amp;#39;s what our vulnerability discovery harness looks like, stage by stage. It was used to scan live code across our runtime, edge data path, protocol stack, control plane, and the open-source projects we depend on.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1OhgJDEeyc5aq8EoF4yFJE/917c9a9d8a92d0920acb96f7d5cb66f6/image6.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;905\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;figure class=\&quot;table table-style-block-align-left\&quot; style=\&quot;width:97.66%;\&quot;&gt;\n    &lt;table class=\&quot;ck-table-resized\&quot; style=\&quot;border-style:none;\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;colgroup&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:11.55%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:56.06%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:32.39%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;/colgroup&gt;\n        &lt;tbody&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Stage&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it does&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why it matters&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5736CBrNDFnYglaRFUtGp0/2bccdc1e5b5f1b21416c6439cdb63967/BLOG-3301_image7.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5736CBrNDFnYglaRFUtGp0/2bccdc1e5b5f1b21416c6439cdb63967/BLOG-3301_image7.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;822rcAA5ctaT\&quot; width=\&quot;32\&quot; height=\&quot;32\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Recon&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An agent reads the repository from the top down, fans out to subagents responsible for each subsystem, and produces an architecture document covering build commands, trust boundaries, entry points, and likely attack surface. It also generates the initial queue of tasks for the next stage.&amp;nbsp;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Gives every downstream agent shared context. Cuts the wander problem.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7wGJ8jmh1gjMevxH31nSfv/b3bd6772cfa57939a1291cae8c4faab3/BLOG-3301_image8.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7wGJ8jmh1gjMevxH31nSfv/b3bd6772cfa57939a1291cae8c4faab3/BLOG-3301_image8.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;MZetX0ROffYM\&quot; width=\&quot;31\&quot; height=\&quot;31\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Hunt&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Each task is one attack class paired with a scope hint. Hunters (the agents that actually look for bugs) run concurrently, typically around fifty at once, each fanning out to a handful of exploration subagents. Each hunter has access to tools that compile and run proof-of-concept code in a per-task scratch directory.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;This is where most of the work happens. Many narrow tasks in parallel, not one exhaustive agent.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7mMu2OOeot8CLuvu2PYAjx/144ecd5ef0785d8c6b0c7c3cd6feccc8/BLOG-3301_image5.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7mMu2OOeot8CLuvu2PYAjx/144ecd5ef0785d8c6b0c7c3cd6feccc8/BLOG-3301_image5.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;b0ZpxOYkUhjr\&quot; width=\&quot;29\&quot; height=\&quot;29\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Validate&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An independent agent re-reads the code and tries to disprove the original finding. It uses a different prompt and has no ability to emit new findings of its own.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Catches a meaningful fraction of the noise the hunter wouldn&#39;t catch when reviewing its own work.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7f8SJhTAja5bLPvydZkrv4/11a9cf6b0cee030db5f56d0dd46e0b45/BLOG-3301_image11.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7f8SJhTAja5bLPvydZkrv4/11a9cf6b0cee030db5f56d0dd46e0b45/BLOG-3301_image11.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;Y31tucc41jy0\&quot; width=\&quot;28\&quot; height=\&quot;28\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Gapfill&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Hunters flag areas they touched but didn&#39;t cover thoroughly. Those areas get re-queued for another pass.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Counteracts the model&#39;s tendency to drift toward attack classes it has already had success with.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5tbCM9SUgCZlYF0m9e8CY0/c48b7f90e39ddc5bd6060d5067bae0ea/BLOG-3301_image4.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5tbCM9SUgCZlYF0m9e8CY0/c48b7f90e39ddc5bd6060d5067bae0ea/BLOG-3301_image4.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;lvNRuZ_yo_es\&quot; width=\&quot;28\&quot; height=\&quot;28\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Dedupe&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Findings that share the same root cause collapse into a single record.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Variant analysis is a feature, not a way to inflate the queue with duplicates.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/51VLQM1a40KKRdvq2HidRj/c9ad0209a54544fe783d9eb019a6f02b/BLOG-3301_image3.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/51VLQM1a40KKRdvq2HidRj/c9ad0209a54544fe783d9eb019a6f02b/BLOG-3301_image3.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;_rUjvs8wRMj1\&quot; width=\&quot;29\&quot; height=\&quot;29\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Trace&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;For each confirmed finding in a shared library, a tracer agent fans out (one instance per consumer repository), uses a cross-repo symbol index, and decides whether attacker-controlled input actually reaches the bug from outside the system.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Turns \&quot;there is a flaw\&quot; into \&quot;there is a reachable vulnerability.\&quot; This is the stage that matters most.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/4UhAgIcdpKcWxNG2kO5fIN/6f883a0f993604339b5381b14f63f40d/BLOG-3301_image1.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/4UhAgIcdpKcWxNG2kO5fIN/6f883a0f993604339b5381b14f63f40d/BLOG-3301_image1.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;ylBXj7IP-Thj\&quot; width=\&quot;26\&quot; height=\&quot;26\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Feedback&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Reachable traces become new hunt tasks in the consumer repositories where the bug is actually exposed.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Closes the loop. The pipeline gets better as it runs.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/558dSiaH5GNrtmKGKbUEUH/8684108e9b99580e5194245a1e20cb79/BLOG-3301_image10.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/558dSiaH5GNrtmKGKbUEUH/8684108e9b99580e5194245a1e20cb79/BLOG-3301_image10.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;pDRKY2-VeUPg\&quot; width=\&quot;27\&quot; height=\&quot;27\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Report&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An agent writes a structured report against a predefined schema, fixes any validation errors against that schema itself, and submits the report to an ingest API.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Output is queryable data, not free-form prose.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n        &lt;/tbody&gt;\n    &lt;/table&gt;\n&lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-this-means-for-security-teams\&quot;&gt;What this means for security teams&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-this-means-for-security-teams\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The loudest reaction to Mythos Preview from other security leaders has been about speed - scan faster, patch faster, compress the response cycle. More than one team we have spoken with is now operating under a two-hour SLA from CVE release to patch in production. The instinct is understandable: when the attacker timeline shortens, the defender timeline has to shorten with it. Faster is not going to be enough, and we think a lot of teams are about to spend a lot of time, effort, and money learning that the hard way.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Patching faster does not change the shape of the pipeline that produces the patch. If regression testing takes a day, you cannot get to a two-hour SLA without skipping it, and the bugs you ship when you skip regression testing tend to be worse than the bugs you were trying to patch. We learned a version of this when we tried letting the model write its own patches and watched a few go out that fixed the original bug while quietly breaking something else the code depended on.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The harder question is what the architecture around the vulnerability should look like. The principle is to make exploitation harder for an attacker even when a bug exists, so that the gap between when a vulnerability is disclosed and when it is patched matters less. That means defenses that sit in front of the application and block the bug from being reached. It means designing the application so that a flaw in one part of the code cannot give an attacker access to other parts. It means being able to roll out a fix to every place the code is running at the same moment, rather than waiting on individual teams to deploy it.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also recognize this topic cuts both ways. The same capabilities that helped us find bugs in our own code will, in the wrong hands, accelerate the attack side against every application on the Internet. Cloudflare sits in front of millions of those applications, and the architectural principles described above are exactly the ones our products are built to apply on behalf of customers. We will share more on what that means for customers in the weeks ahead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If your team is doing similar work and would like to compare notes, reach out to us at &lt;a href=\&quot;#\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;security-ai-research@cloudflare.com&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;Our research with Mythos Preview was conducted in a controlled environment against our own code; every vulnerability surfaced through this work was triaged, validated, and remediated where action was needed under Cloudflare&amp;#39;s formal vulnerability management process.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;This work was a team effort. Thanks to Albert Pedersen, Craig Strubhart, Dan Jones, Irtefa Fairuz, Martin Schwarzl, and Rohit Chenna Reddy for their contributions to the research, engineering, and analysis behind this blog Cloudflareâs Security and Engineering teams began assessing the vulnerability as soon as it was disclosed. We reviewed the exploit technique, evaluated exposure across our infrastructure, and validated that our existing behavioral detections could identify the exploit pattern within minutes.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;There was no impact to the Cloudflare environment, no customer data was at risk, and no services were disrupted at any point.&lt;/b&gt; Read on to learn how our preparedness paid off.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;background\&quot;&gt;Background&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#background\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    \n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;our-linux-kernel-release-process\&quot;&gt;Our Linux kernel release process&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#our-linux-kernel-release-process\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare operates a global Linux server infrastructure at an immense scale, with datacenters located &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/network/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;across 330 cities&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We maintain a custom Linux kernel build based on the community&amp;#39;s Long-Term Support (LTS) versions to manage updates effectively at this volume. At any given time, we may utilize multiple LTS versions from various series, such as 6.12 or 6.18, which benefit from extended update periods.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The community regularly merges and releases security and stability updates which trigger an automated job to generate a new internal kernel build approximately every week. These builds undergo testing in our staging data centers to ensure stability before a global rollout. Following a successful release, the Edge Reboot Release (ERR) pipeline manages a systematic update and reboot of the edge infrastructure on a four-week cycle. Our control plane infrastructure typically adopts the most recent kernel, with reboots scheduled according to specific workload requirements.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;By the time a CVE becomes public knowledge, the necessary fix has typically been integrated into stable Linux LTS releases for several weeks. Our established procedures ensure that we have already deployed these patches.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At the time of the &amp;quot;Copy Fail&amp;quot; disclosure, the majority of our infrastructure was running the 6.12 LTS version, while a subset of machines had begun transitioning to the newer 6.18 LTS release.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;about-the-copy-fail-vulnerability\&quot;&gt;About the Copy Fail vulnerability&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#about-the-copy-fail-vulnerability\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;It helps to understand the vulnerability before getting to the response story. A comprehensive write-up can be found in the original &lt;a href=\&quot;https://xint.io/blog/copy-fail-linux-distributions\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Xint Code disclosure&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; post.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;af_alg-and-the-kernel-crypto-api\&quot;&gt;AF_ALG and the kernel crypto API&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#af_alg-and-the-kernel-crypto-api\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Linux kernel&amp;#39;s internal crypto API manages functions like kTLS and IPsec. Userspace programs access this via the &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; socket family, allowing unprivileged processes to request encryption or decryption. The &lt;code&gt;algif_aead&lt;/code&gt; module facilitates this for Authenticated Encryption with Associated Data (AEAD) ciphers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;An unprivileged program follows these steps:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Opens an &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; socket and binds to an AEAD template.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Sets a key and accepts a request socket.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Submits input via &lt;code&gt;sendmsg()&lt;/code&gt; or &lt;code&gt;splice()&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Executes the operation using &lt;code&gt;recvmsg()&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;splice()&lt;/code&gt; system call is critical here, as it moves data by passing page cache references.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;memory-mechanics-page-cache-and-in-place-crypto\&quot;&gt;Memory mechanics: page cache and in-place crypto&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#memory-mechanics-page-cache-and-in-place-crypto\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The &lt;b&gt;page cache&lt;/b&gt; is a shared system cache for file contents. Modifying a page belonging to a setuid binary effectively edits that program for all users until the page is evicted.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The crypto API utilizes &lt;b&gt;scatterlists&lt;/b&gt;, which are structures linking various memory pages. In 2017, &lt;code&gt;algif_aead&lt;/code&gt; was optimized for &lt;i&gt;in-place&lt;/i&gt; operations, chaining destination and reference pages together. This design lacked enforcement to prevent algorithms from writing past intended boundaries.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;the-vulnerability-out-of-bounds-write\&quot;&gt;The vulnerability: out-of-bounds write&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-vulnerability-out-of-bounds-write\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When the user executes &lt;code&gt;recvmsg()&lt;/code&gt;, the &lt;code&gt;authencesn&lt;/code&gt; wrapper in the kernel performs a 4-byte write past the legitimate output region:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-c++\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-c++\&quot;&gt;scatterwalk_map_and_copy(tmp + 1, dst, assoclen + cryptlen, 4, 1);\n&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;By using &lt;code&gt;splice()&lt;/code&gt;, an attacker can chain a target file&amp;#39;s page cache pages to the scatterlist. The out-of-bounds write then taints the cached file, allowing an attacker to control which file is modified, the offset, and the specific 4 bytes written. This means the attacker can manipulate the following with this exploit:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;File: Any readable file.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Offset: Tunable via &lt;code&gt;assoclen&lt;/code&gt; and splice parameters.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Value: Controlled via AAD bytes 4-7 in &lt;code&gt;sendmsg()&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;the-exploit-step-by-step\&quot;&gt;The exploit, step by step&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-exploit-step-by-step\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    \n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5uqfOddH7biQaTjtgQOist/5c16c08bf3e5ce2f9030d6f98d2403cb/BLOG-3308_2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3308 2\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1106\&quot; height=\&quot;900\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;The default exploit targets &lt;code&gt;/usr/bin/su&lt;/code&gt;, a setuid-root binary present on essentially every distribution.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Cache Reference:&lt;/b&gt; Open &lt;code&gt;/usr/bin/su&lt;/code&gt; as &lt;code&gt;O_RDONLY&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;read()&lt;/code&gt; to populate the page cache. Use splice() on the file descriptor to pass these page cache references into the crypto scatterlist.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Setup:&lt;/b&gt; Create an &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; socket, &lt;code&gt;bind()&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;authencesn(hmac(sha256),cbc(aes))&lt;/code&gt;, set a key, and accept a request socket without needing privileges.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Write Construction:&lt;/b&gt; For each 4-byte shellcode chunk:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;sendmsg()&lt;/code&gt; with AAD bytes 4â7 containing the shellcode.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;code&gt;splice()&lt;/code&gt; the binary into a pipe then the &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; socket so &lt;code&gt;assoclen + cryptlen&lt;/code&gt; targets the desired &lt;code&gt;.text&lt;/code&gt; offset.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Trigger:&lt;/b&gt; &lt;code&gt;recvmsg()&lt;/code&gt; initiates decryption. &lt;code&gt;authencesn&lt;/code&gt; writes its scratch data to the target offset of &lt;code&gt;/usr/bin/su&lt;/code&gt; in the page cache. Although the function returns &lt;code&gt;-EBADMSG&lt;/code&gt;, the 4-byte write is now in the global page cache.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Execution:&lt;/b&gt; Running &lt;code&gt;execve(&amp;quot;/usr/bin/su&amp;quot;)&lt;/code&gt; loads the tainted page cache. Since the binary is setuid-root, the injected shellcode executes with &lt;b&gt;root&lt;/b&gt; privileges.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;The upstream fix (&lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/torvalds/linux/commit/a664bf3d603d\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;commit a664bf3d603d&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;) reverts the 2017 in-place optimization, removing the exploit.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;how-we-responded\&quot;&gt;How we respondedÂ &lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-we-responded\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When the vulnerability was disclosed, many workstreams started in parallel:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Mapping the blast radius:&lt;/b&gt; Our security team worked with kernel engineers to determine which kernel versions were vulnerable and assess the potential exposure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Validating coverage:&lt;/b&gt; Security reviewed the exploit technique and confirmed that our existing behavioral detections could identify the exploit pattern during authorized internal validation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Proactive threat hunting:&lt;/b&gt; Security began searching for signs that the vulnerability had been exploited before it was publicly known, going back 48 hours in our fleet-wide logs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Engineering a mitigation:&lt;/b&gt; Kernel engineers began building a runtime mitigation that would protect the fleet without breaking production services.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Continuing software updates&lt;/b&gt;: Our engineering teams worked on delivering an updated Linux kernel, which required carefully rebooting and rolling it out across our servers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;There was no customer impact at any point during this response.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;validating-detection-coverage\&quot;&gt;Validating detection coverage&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#validating-detection-coverage\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One of the first things our security team did was confirm that our existing endpoint detection would catch this exploit. Our servers run behavioral detection that continuously monitors process execution patterns. It doesn&amp;#39;t rely on knowing about specific vulnerabilities; it watches for anomalous behavior across the fleet.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When our engineers validated the vulnerability internally as part of the response, the detection platform flagged it within minutes. The system linked the entire execution chainâstarting at the script interpreter, moving through the kernelâs cryptographic subsystem, and ending at the privilege escalation binaryâflagging it as malicious based on fleet-wide behavioral patterns.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This happened without a signature update, without a rule change, and without human intervention. Our behavioral detection coverage existed before we wrote any custom logic for this particular Copy File exploit.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The confirmation was important because it meant we had coverage before writing a vulnerability-specific rule.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;hunting-for-exploitation\&quot;&gt;Hunting for exploitation&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#hunting-for-exploitation\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;While our engineering team moved to a more targeted mitigation, our security investigation had been running since disclosure. This is our standard procedure for any critical vulnerability.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our security team operates on a simple principle for critical vulnerabilities: assume compromise until you can prove otherwise. The investigation started from the assumption that exploitation could have occurred before the vulnerability was public, and we worked systematically to either confirm or rule it out.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The exploit leaves a distinctive trace in kernel logs when it runs. We searched for that trace across our centralized logging infrastructure, covering 48 hours before the vulnerability was publicly disclosed. If someone had exploited this before the world knew about it, we would have seen it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We pulled access logs for affected systems and reconstructed who connected, when, and what commands they ran. This gave us a complete forensic picture of interactive activity on potentially affected infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We checked that system binaries had not been tampered with, validated cryptographic hashes against known-good package manifests, looked for persistence mechanisms, and audited network connections for anything unusual. Everything was clean. &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;incident-timeline-and-impact\&quot;&gt;Incident timeline and impact&lt;/h2&gt;\n Therefore, the simple fix was to just remove this module and disallow it from being reloaded.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This mitigation was therefore exactly what the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://copy.fail/\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    \n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2VYFKst8aUkkHCaEdb8yQ4/91ad9a855d6185bccd179fcf092ed636/BLOG-3308_4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3308 4\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1300\&quot; height=\&quot;898\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâve already developed and deployed such a tool for this exact scenario: &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/live-patch-security-vulnerabilities-with-ebpf-lsm/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;bpf-lsm&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Instead of removing the module, this tool leaves it loaded for legitimate users and uses a BPF Linux Security Module program to deny the &lt;code&gt;socket_bind&lt;/code&gt; LSM hook for everyone else. This completely blocks the front door for any exploits.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;A draft of the eBPF program was put together overnight. Team members picked it up the following morning, ran validations, and made it production-ready. The program is fairly straightforward. On every &lt;code&gt;socket_bind&lt;/code&gt; call:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;If the socket family is not &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt;, allow the call through unchanged.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;If the family is &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt;, check the calling binary&amp;#39;s path against an allow-list of the binaries we know to be legitimate users.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;If the binary is on the allow-list, allow the bind. Otherwise, deny it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;To verify the mitigation on a given machine without exploiting it, the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://copy.fail/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Copy Fail&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; write-up gives a one-liner:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-shell\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-shell\&quot;&gt;python3 -c &amp;#039;import socket; s = socket.socket(socket.AF_ALG, socket.SOCK_SEQPACKET, 0); s.bind((&amp;quot;aead&amp;quot;,&amp;quot;authencesn(hmac(sha256),cbc(aes))&amp;quot;));&amp;#039;&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;On a mitigated machine you get PermissionError: [Errno 1] Operation not permitted (or FileNotFoundError, depending on which mitigation is active) instead of a successful bind.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;rolling-it-out\&quot;&gt;Rolling it out&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#rolling-it-out\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Before enabling enforcement, we verified that our known internal service was the sole legitimate &lt;/b&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;b&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;b&gt; user to avoid accidental outages.&lt;/b&gt; We used &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/ebpf_exporter\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;prometheus-ebpf-exporter&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to hook the &lt;code&gt;socket()&lt;/code&gt; syscall and track &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; usage per binary across the fleet. This required no kernel changes and provided aggregate data from hundreds of thousands of servers within hours. Results confirmed the identified service was indeed the only legitimate user.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;So the bpf-lsm rollout was deliberately staged in two steps:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Get visibility first.&lt;/b&gt; Push the ebpf-exporter config gated by salt. Confirm at the metric layer that the known service is effectively the only thing creating &lt;code&gt;AF_ALG&lt;/code&gt; sockets.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Then enforce.&lt;/b&gt; Push the bpf-lsm program behind a separate enforcement gate.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ol&gt;&lt;p&gt;In parallel, the upstream backport for our majority LTS line finally became available, and our internal automation built a patched kernel against it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We started to test the patched kernel in our staging datacenters as soon as possible, then we resumed the longer reboot process in order to fully patch our fleet.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;remediation-and-follow-up-steps\&quot;&gt;Remediation and follow-up steps&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#remediation-and-follow-up-steps\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;While we were prepared for this scenario, at Cloudflare weâre always learning and improving. Key areas we identified for improvement:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Better visibility into kernel-API dependencies.&lt;/b&gt; We will review kernel-subsystem usage across production services, so we can continue to quickly mitigate exploits without service disruption.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Better runtime mitigation.&lt;/b&gt; bpf-lsm is a valuable tool for mitigations, but we want to make this tool even better. This will include looking into faster deployments, better playbooks, and better logging and visibility of the tool.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Reduce attack surface of Linux Kernel&lt;/b&gt;. Review and audit our kernel configuration. Proactively identify unused modules or features so that we can remove them from our build entirely.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;conclusion\&quot;&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#conclusion\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The &amp;quot;Copy Fail&amp;quot; vulnerability presented a unique challenge for us. Despite our practice of deploying Linux patch updates every two weeks, we remained vulnerable because a month-old mainline fix had yet to be backported to our primary kernel line. Despite that, we were still able to roll out patched kernels within hours of the backport&amp;#39;s release. In the interim, bpf-lsm provided a surgical, no-reboot mitigation that secured our fleet. While our initial attempt to disable the problematic module failed, it did so safely within our internal staging environment rather than production, allowing us to identify this dependency.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;By the end of the rollout, every machine in our fleet was protected by either a patched kernel or a bpf-lsm program denying the vulnerable code path to non-allow-listed binaries. There was no customer impact at any point during this incident, and we have committed to the follow-up work above to make our response faster and our visibility better the next time something like this lands. Responsible disclosure works, in-kernel visibility tooling pays off in moments exactly like this one, and bpf-lsm continues to be one of the most useful primitives we have for runtime kernel mitigation.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, critical vulnerability response is a coordinated effort across Security, Engineering, Product, and many other teams. Special thanks to Ali Adnan, Ivan Babrou, Frederik Baetens, Curtis Bray, Piers Cornwell, Everton Didone Foscarini, Rob Dinh, Elle Dougherty, Kevin Flansburg, Matt Fleming, Kimberley Hall, Brandon Harris, Jerry Ho, Oxana Kharitonova, Marek Kroemeke, Fred Lawler, James Munson, Nafeez Nazer, Walead Parviz, Miguel Pato, Evan Pratten, Josh Seba, June Slater, Ryan Timken, Michael Wolf, Jianxin Zeng and everyone else who contributed to the investigation, mitigation, and remediation of Copy Fail. We&amp;#39;d also like to thank the Linux upstream maintainers and Copy Fail researchers whose work helped make a rapid response
